========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Hi, Dr. Smith. Um, well, I guess I'm here because my manager told me I had to come see someone. It's just... what scared everyone at work is that I started freezing up in the middle of service. Or I'd be doing things totally out of order. 

Which is weird because I've been waitressing for a really long time, ever since I paused my classes at the community college. I was taking this art history seminar back then, and the professor had this really annoying habit of clicking his pen against his teeth. Click, click, click. It drove me crazy, but I loved learning about how they used to mix their own oil paints back in the Renaissance. They used lapis lazuli for blue, you know? It had to be imported and was so expensive they only used it for the Virgin Mary's robes. 

But anyway, at the restaurant, my manager got mad because I'd just be standing by the soda station... holding the cash drawer key and just repeating table numbers out loud instead of actually closing out the checks. I don't really know why I was doing that.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, let me think... I guess this whole change in me has been building for about a year and two months. That's right around when my manager asked me to take on the evening shift lead responsibilities. 

The evening shift is so different from the morning one, honestly. In the mornings, you get all the quiet regulars. We had this one older man who came in every Tuesday, and he would always leave exactly a dollar and forty-five cents as a tip, no matter what he ordered. He used to fold these little origami cranes out of the morning newspaper while he waited for his toast. I actually kept one on the dashboard of my Honda for the longest time, until the sun faded it so bad you couldn't read the print on the paper anymore. I think the article he used was about local zoning laws? I don't really follow city council stuff, it always just seems like people arguing about property lines and parking permits. My neighbor actually put up a chain-link fence last month that is definitely a foot over my property line, but I haven't said anything to him because he has this really aggressive terrier that just barks at the wind... 

Wait, I'm sorry. What did you ask me? Oh, right, the freezing up at work. Yeah, it's been about a year and two months since I became shift lead.

Um... yeah, actually. I guess there is something like that. Sometimes, especially when I'm closing up the restaurant or even just when I'm home in my own kitchen, I'll hear this woman's voice. It's really frustrating when I'm trying to count the drawer, honestly. The coins are always the worst part. We have this one cashier, Brenda, who always mixes the quarters and the nickels in the same little compartment, and it drives me absolutely insane. I mean, they're completely different sizes, how do you even do that by accident? Brenda is... well, she's nice, but she's always talking about her cats. She has five of them, and she knits these little sweaters for them. I tried knitting once, back when I was taking that art history class. I thought it would be relaxing, you know? But the yarn kept getting tangled, and my tension was all wrong. My grandmother used to knit these beautiful afghans. She made me this gorgeous yellow one when I graduated high school. It was the exact shade of yellow as a school bus. I always wondered why they painted school buses that specific color. I think I read somewhere it was for visibility, especially in the fog. Fog can be so dangerous to drive in. 

But yeah, the voice... it's just this woman, and she sounds so real, but then nobody else reacts.

just normal restaurant mess-ups.\n- key: S020\n  name: Hallucinations\n  description: Perception-like experiences that occur without an external stimulus and are as vivid and clear as normal perception.\n  manifestation: Sometimes during closing or when I’m home in the kitchen, I hear a woman’s voice say my name or tell me I’m doing the count wrong, clear enough that I answer before I realize nobody else heard it.\n- key: S022\n  name: Disorganized_Abnormal_Behavior\n  description: A state where behavior and motor control are severely disturbed, appearing as movements or reactions inappropriate to the situation.\n  manifestation: What scared everyone at work is that I started freezing up in the middle of service or doing things out of order, like standing by the soda station holding the cash drawer key and repeating table numbers instead of closing checks.\n- key: S023\n  name: Negative_Symptoms\n  description: A state where emotions, motivation, speech, or social interest that should normally be present are diminished or lost.\n  manifestation: I don’t really go for beach walks, bake, garden, or write in my journal much anymore, and when my husband tries to talk with me I mostly just sit there because it feels like I can’t get myself to care or start anything.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I’m close to losing the shift-lead opportunity because my mistakes and confusion have disrupted service and closing, and at home my husband says I’m barely present with him anymore.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 2 months\n  manifestation: This whole change in me has been building for about a year and two months, since I was asked to take on the evening shift lead responsibilities.\n- key: psychotic_symptoms_duration\n  name: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\n  description: 4 months\n  manifestation: The clearer voices and firm beliefs about coworkers and customers targeting me have been happening for about four months.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 32 / waiter_or_waitress / some_college\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S022\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome in. I'm Dr. Smith. To start, can you tell me what brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, Dr. Smith. Um, well, I guess I'm here because my manager told me I had to come see someone. It's just... what scared everyone at work is that I started freezing up in the middle of service. Or I'd be doing things totally out of order. \n\nWhich is weird because I've been waitressing for a really long time, ever since I paused my classes at the community college. I was taking this art history seminar back then, and the professor had this really annoying habit of clicking his pen against his teeth. Click, click, click. It drove me crazy, but I loved learning about how they used to mix their own oil paints back in the Renaissance. They used lapis lazuli for blue, you know? It had to be imported and was so expensive they only used it for the Virgin Mary's robes. \n\nBut anyway, at the restaurant, my manager got mad because I'd just be standing by the soda station... holding the cash drawer key and just repeating table numbers out loud instead of actually closing out the checks. I don't really know why I was doing that.\n\nDoctor: How long has this been going on—when did you first notice yourself freezing up or losing track of what you were doing during your shifts?\n\nPatient: Um, let me think... I guess this whole change in me has been building for about a year and two months. That's right around when my manager asked me to take on the evening shift lead responsibilities. \n\nThe evening shift is so different from the morning one, honestly. In the mornings, you get all the quiet regulars. We had this one older man who came in every Tuesday, and he would always leave exactly a dollar and forty-five cents as a tip, no matter what he ordered. He used to fold these little origami cranes out of the morning newspaper while he waited for his toast. I actually kept one on the dashboard of my Honda for the longest time, until the sun faded it so bad you couldn't read the print on the paper anymore. I think the article he used was about local zoning laws? I don't really follow city council stuff, it always just seems like people arguing about property lines and parking permits. My neighbor actually put up a chain-link fence last month that is definitely a foot over my property line, but I haven't said anything to him because he has this really aggressive terrier that just barks at the wind... \n\nWait, I'm sorry. What did you ask me? Oh, right, the freezing up at work. Yeah, it's been about a year and two months since I became shift lead.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices, seen things others don't see, or had beliefs that others found unusual or hard to believe, even before the freezing episodes started?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, actually. I guess there is something like that. Sometimes, especially when I'm closing up the restaurant or even just when I'm home in my own kitchen, I'll hear this woman's voice. She'll just say my name, or sometimes she tells me I'm doing the drawer count wrong. It's so clear that I actually answer her out loud before I realize nobody is there. \n\nIt's really frustrating when I'm trying to count the drawer, honestly. The coins are always the worst part. We have this one cashier, Brenda, who always mixes the quarters and the nickels in the same little compartment, and it drives me absolutely insane. I mean, they're completely different sizes, how do you even do that by accident? Brenda is... well, she's nice, but she's always talking about her cats. She has five of them, and she knits these little sweaters for them. I tried knitting once, back when I was taking that art history class. I thought it would be relaxing, you know? But the yarn kept getting tangled, and my tension was all wrong. My grandmother used to knit these beautiful afghans. She made me this gorgeous yellow one when I graduated high school. It was the exact shade of yellow as a school bus. I always wondered why they painted school buses that specific color. I think I read somewhere it was for visibility, especially in the fog. Fog can be so dangerous to drive in. \n\nBut yeah, the voice... it's just this woman, and she sounds so real, but then nobody else reacts.\n\n[Last Question]\nAside from the freezing episodes and the voice you hear, have you noticed any periods lasting a week or more where your mood was unusually high, irritable, or energetic, or alternatively, where you felt very depressed, hopeless, or lost interest in things?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. Um... no, not really. I don't think I've had any weeks where I felt super high or energetic, or really depressed either. I mean, my mood is just kind of... normal, I guess? Well, as normal as it can be when you're on your feet for eight hours a day. 

You know, the mats behind the counter at work are supposed to help with that. They're those thick, black rubber ones with the holes in them? But honestly, I think they just make it worse sometimes because if you drop a pen or a coin, it falls right through the holes and you have to lift the whole heavy mat up to get it out. The floors underneath are always so disgusting, too. It's like this sticky mix of spilled syrup and mop water. We use this industrial floor cleaner that smells exactly like artificial pine needles. It reminds me of those little cardboard air fresheners people hang from their rearview mirrors. My dad used to have one in his old pickup truck, the one he used to haul firewood. He chopped all his own wood for the winter. He said it warmed you twice—once when you chopped it, and once when you burned it. I always thought that was a funny saying. We had this huge cast-iron woodstove in the living room...

But, um, yeah. No long periods of feeling really depressed or too energetic. Just regular.

That's right around when my manager asked me to take on the evening shift lead responsibilities. \n\nThe evening shift is so different from the morning one, honestly. In the mornings, you get all the quiet regulars. We had this one older man who came in every Tuesday, and he would always leave exactly a dollar and forty-five cents as a tip, no matter what he ordered. He used to fold these little origami cranes out of the morning newspaper while he waited for his toast. I actually kept one on the dashboard of my Honda for the longest time, until the sun faded it so bad you couldn't read the print on the paper anymore. I think the article he used was about local zoning laws? I don't really follow city council stuff, it always just seems like people arguing about property lines and parking permits. My neighbor actually put up a chain-link fence last month that is definitely a foot over my property line, but I haven't said anything to him because he has this really aggressive terrier that just barks at the wind... \n\nWait, I'm sorry. What did you ask me? Oh, right, the freezing up at work. Yeah, it's been about a year and two months since I became shift lead.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices, seen things others don't see, or had beliefs that others found unusual or hard to believe, even before the freezing episodes started?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, actually. I guess there is something like that. Sometimes, especially when I'm closing up the restaurant or even just when I'm home in my own kitchen, I'll hear this woman's voice. She'll just say my name, or sometimes she tells me I'm doing the drawer count wrong. It's so clear that I actually answer her out loud before I realize nobody is there. \n\nIt's really frustrating when I'm trying to count the drawer, honestly. The coins are always the worst part. We have this one cashier, Brenda, who always mixes the quarters and the nickels in the same little compartment, and it drives me absolutely insane. She has five of them, and she knits these little sweaters for them. I tried knitting once, back when I was taking that art history class. I thought it would be relaxing, you know? But the yarn kept getting tangled, and my tension was all wrong. My grandmother used to knit these beautiful afghans. She made me this gorgeous yellow one when I graduated high school. It was the exact shade of yellow as a school bus. I always wondered why they painted school buses that specific color. I think I read somewhere it was for visibility, especially in the fog. Fog can be so dangerous to drive in. \n\nBut yeah, the voice... it's just this woman, and she sounds so real, but then nobody else reacts.\n\nDoctor: Aside from the freezing episodes and the voice you hear, have you noticed any periods lasting a week or more where your mood was unusually high, irritable, or energetic, or alternatively, where you felt very depressed, hopeless, or lost interest in things?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I don't think I've had any weeks where I felt super high or energetic, or really depressed either. I mean, my mood is just kind of... normal, I guess? Well, as normal as it can be when you're on your feet for eight hours a day. \n\nYou know, the mats behind the counter at work are supposed to help with that. They're those thick, black rubber ones with the holes in them? But honestly, I think they just make it worse sometimes because if you drop a pen or a coin, it falls right through the holes and you have to lift the whole heavy mat up to get it out. The floors underneath are always so disgusting, too. It's like this sticky mix of spilled syrup and mop water. We use this industrial floor cleaner that smells exactly like artificial pine needles. It reminds me of those little cardboard air fresheners people hang from their rearview mirrors. My dad used to have one in his old pickup truck, the one he used to haul firewood. He chopped all his own wood for the winter. He said it warmed you twice—once when you chopped it, and once when you burned it. I always thought that was a funny saying. We had this huge cast-iron woodstove in the living room...\n\nBut, um, yeah. No long periods of feeling really depressed or too energetic. Just regular.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you had any recent head injuries, seizures, significant illnesses, or been using any drugs, alcohol, or new medications around the time these freezing episodes and the voice started?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, no. No head injuries or seizures or anything like that. I don't really drink either, and I haven't started any new medications. I just take some ibuprofen sometimes if my back hurts from standing. 

The ibuprofen bottles always have those child-proof caps, you know? The ones where you have to push down and twist at the exact same time. I swear they're adult-proof too, sometimes I can barely get them open when my hands are tired. My hands get so dry from washing them constantly at work. The soap dispenser in the employee bathroom is always filled with this really harsh pink liquid soap. It smells like cheap bubblegum. I actually hate the smell of bubblegum. When I was a kid, I swallowed a piece of bubblegum on a dare, and my older brother told me it would stay in my stomach for seven years. I was terrified for months that it was just sitting in there, growing into a giant pink ball. He used to tell me all sorts of things like that. Once he convinced me that if you ate watermelon seeds, a vine would grow out of your ears. We used to have these huge seed-spitting contests in the backyard during the summer... 

But yeah, no head injuries or new drugs or anything like that. Just the ibuprofen for my back.

He chopped all his own wood for the winter. He said it warmed you twice—once when you chopped it, and once when you burned it. I always thought that was a funny saying. Um, let me think... yeah, it's definitely been more than six months. I'd say this whole change in me, the voice and just feeling kind of scattered... it's been building for about a year and two months. It all started right around the time they asked me to take on the evening shift lead responsibilities at the restaurant. 

Being shift lead is actually a lot more stressful than they make it sound. You get this tiny bump in pay, like maybe fifty cents an hour, but suddenly you're responsible for making sure everyone does their sidework. And nobody ever wants to do the ketchup bottles. You have to marry the ketchup—that's what we call it when you pour the half-empty bottles into each other so they look full for the next day. It's so messy, and if you don't wipe the rims perfectly, they get all crusty overnight. I hate crusty ketchup. It reminds me of when my little nephew comes over. He's four, and he absolutely covers everything in ketchup. Eggs, mac and cheese, even green beans. My sister says it's just a phase, but I don't know. When I was four, I only wanted to eat things that were orange. Carrots, cheddar cheese, cantaloupe... I refused to eat anything else. My mom had to sneak vitamins into my juice just to make sure I wasn't getting scurvy or something. Scurvy is such a weird word, isn't it? It sounds like a pirate thing. I watched this documentary about pirates once, and they actually drank a lot of lime juice to prevent it. That's why British sailors were called limeys...

But yeah, it's been about a year and two months, since the evening shift lead thing started.

I watched this documentary about pirates once, and they actually drank a lot of lime juice to prevent it. That's why British sailors were called limeys...\n\nBut yeah, it's been about a year and two months, since the evening shift lead thing started."

It had to be imported and was so expensive they only used it for the Virgin Mary's robes. \n\nBut anyway, at the restaurant, my manager got mad because I'd just be standing by the soda station... holding the cash drawer key and just repeating table numbers out loud instead of actually closing out the checks. Subtypes: Fasting, Excessive Exercise\n[S048] Compensatory_Behavior_Purging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors involving the expulsion of food or fluids to prevent weight gain.  Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight. Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge. Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.
